        "end_char": 143,
        "text": "MEXICO\nB. External Debt Sustainability\nMexico’s external debt as a ratio to GDP is comparatively low and is projected to decline further below\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00002",
        "start_char": 143,
        "end_char": 344,
        "text": "its pre-pandemic level in 2022. The decline is mainly due to the economic rebound after the pandemic,\nbut also due to continued excess capacity and strong remittance inflows, which have contributed to\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00003",
        "start_char": 344,
        "end_char": 536,
        "text": "small current account deficits. The main risks to the external debt trajectory relate to growth\nunderperformance, higher risk premia, and large currency depreciation. However, their potential\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00004",
        "start_char": 536,
        "end_char": 760,
        "text": "impact is mitigated by several factors, especially favorable maturity and currency structures of public\nand private external debt.\n1. The baseline projections: Gross external debt is expected to decline to around 32 percent\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00005",
        "start_char": 760,
        "end_char": 954,
        "text": "of GDP by end-2022, below the pre-pandemic ratio of 37 percent of GDP. The decline is mainly\ndriven by strong nominal GDP growth (in U.S. dollar terms), but also by robust remittances at levels\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00006",
        "start_char": 954,
        "end_char": 1103,
        "text": "well above recent historical averages. In the medium-term, the external debt ratio is expected to\nremain broadly stable at around 32 percent of GDP.\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00007",
        "start_char": 1103,
        "end_char": 1285,
        "text": "2. Risks and mitigating factors: The major downside risks to the external debt trajectory are\nweaker-than-expected growth, possibly owing to a global slowdown and/or domestic policy\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00008",
        "start_char": 1285,
        "end_char": 1481,
        "text": "missteps, and a sharp tightening of global financial conditions, leading to a spike in risk premia on\nMexico’s external debt liabilities, a weaker peso, and more capital flow volatility. Currency\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00009",
        "start_char": 1481,
        "end_char": 1684,
        "text": "depreciation is a significant risk; for example, a 30 percent depreciation of the peso in real effective\nterms could raise the external debt to about 46 percent of GDP. However, several factors mitigate\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00010",
        "start_char": 1684,
        "end_char": 1884,
        "text": "the impact of potential shocks on Mexico’s external debt position. Rollover risks for the public sector\nexternal debt, which constitute around two-thirds of Mexico’s external debt, are mitigated by a\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00011",
        "start_char": 1884,
        "end_char": 2084,
        "text": "favorable maturity structure (more than 90 percent of debt has a residual maturity of more than one\nyear), currency composition (around 30 percent of external public debt is denominated in peso), and\n"
      },
      {
        "segment_id": "S00012",
